Q1. Digit sum \(9\) with tens digit \(5\) identifies which two-digit number?
A. \(53\)
B. \(56\)
C. \(54\)
D. \(55\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
5+u=9\Rightarrow u=4
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 54.

\[
N=10\times 5+4=54
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 54 is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=4
\]

\[
N=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. The tens place has \(7\), and all digits add to \(9\). Which number is it?
A. \(71\)
B. \(74\)
C. \(72\)
D. \(73\)

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
7+u=9\Rightarrow u=2
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 72.

\[
N=10\times 7+2=72
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 72 is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(72\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=2
\]

\[
N=72
\]

Fast result: \(72\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_missing_digit_multi_rule

Q3. The number \(714x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q4. Between \(139\) and \(282\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 139 is 150.

\[
15\times 10=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 282.

\[
282=15\times 18+12
\]

\[
282-12=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-150}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{282}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{138}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-9=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
---
Q5. Between \(209\) and \(365\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 209 is 210.

\[
15\times 14=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 365.

\[
365=15\times 24+5
\]

\[
365-5=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{360-210}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{365}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{208}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-13=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q6. Between \(109\) and \(198\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 109 is 117.

\[
9\times 13=117
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 198 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 198.

\[
198=9\times 22+0
\]

\[
198-0=198
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{198-117}{9}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{198}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{108}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-12=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q7. Bells ring at intervals of \(78\) and \(78\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(77\)
B. \(80\)
C. \(78\)
D. \(79\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=78
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 78.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(78\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=\frac{78\times 78}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,78)}=78
\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge
---
Q8. For the number \(480\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(480)=(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=24
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 24.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_hidden_divisor_deduction
---

Q14. For the number \(1440\), find the count of odd divisors?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

\[
O=6
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_odd_even_divisor_count
---
Q15. For the number \(1440\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(5897\)
B. \(4423\)
C. \(4914\)
D. \(5405\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(1440)=4914
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 4914.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4914\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(4914\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q16. For the number \(20\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(9600\)
B. \(7200\)
C. \(8000\)
D. \(8800\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
20=2^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(20)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 20 d=20^{d(20)/2}=20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 8000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---
Q17. Bells ring at intervals of \(165\) and \(150\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(1980\)
B. \(1485\)
C. \(1650\)
D. \(1815\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
165=3\times 5\times 11
\]

\[
150=2\times 3\times 5^{2}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,150)=1650
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1650.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1650\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,150)=\frac{165\times 150}{\operatorname{HCF}(165,150)}=1650
\]

Fast result: \(1650\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q25. When \(13^{68}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(68-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{68}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(68-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
6^{68}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division
---
Q26. When \(11^{45}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(45-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
11^{45}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(45-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[
2^{45}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_remainder_after_power
---
Q27. Power \(7^{90}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(90-1)\bmod 10+1=10
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{90}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 10th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(90-1)\bmod 10+1=10
\]

\[
7^{90}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q29. When \(13^{25}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(25-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
13^{25}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(25-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{25}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q41. Tens digit \(7\) and ones digit \(9\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(17\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+9=79
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+7=97
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-9|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-9|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(14\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

Trailing zeroes come from factor pairs 2×5; count fives.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
12+2=14
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 60! = 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q52. Between \(146\) and \(314\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(27\)
B. \(30\)
C. \(28\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 146 is 150.

\[
6\times 25=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 314.

\[
314=6\times 52+2
\]

\[
314-2=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{312-150}{6}+1=27+1=28
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 28.
Hence, there are 28 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{314}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{145}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
52-24=28
\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q53. Bells ring at intervals of \(30\) and \(36\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(198\)
B. \(171\)
C. \(180\)
D. \(189\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(30,36)=180
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 180.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(180\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(30,36)=\frac{30\times 36}{\operatorname{HCF}(30,36)}=180
\]

Fast result: \(180\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q88. The number \(64x29\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=9
\]

\[
9+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
9+x=11
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
9+2=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q91. Power \(3^{57}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(57-1)\bmod 5+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
3^{57}\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(57-1)\bmod 5+1=2
\]

\[
3^{57}\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q104. Between \(124\) and \(282\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 124 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 282.

\[
282=18\times 15+12
\]

\[
282-12=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{270-126}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{282}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{123}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-6=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\left\lfloor\frac{50}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
25+12+6+3+1=47
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 50! is 47.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(47\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(47\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q113. Between \(108\) and \(214\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 108 is 108.

\[
6\times 18=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 210 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 214.

\[
214=6\times 35+4
\]

\[
214-4=210
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{210-108}{6}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{214}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{107}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
35-17=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q114. Reduce the power \(7^{91}\) by its remainder pattern. What is the remainder on division by \(19\)?
A. \(8\)

C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(91-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{91}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(91-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
7^{91}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q136. The number \(7x216\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Final Answer

Q149. The number \(56x41\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-4
\]

\[
-4+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-4+x=0
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-4+4=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q165. Between \(220\) and \(323\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 220 is 234.

\[
18\times 13=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 323.

\[
323=18\times 17+17
\]

\[
323-17=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{306-234}{18}+1=4+1=5
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 5.
Hence, there are 5 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{323}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{219}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-12=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q180. Between \(210\) and \(350\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 210 is 216.

\[
8\times 27=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 344 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 350.

\[
350=8\times 43+6
\]

\[
350-6=344
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{344-216}{8}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{350}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{209}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
43-26=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q181. Two cyclic events repeat every \(156\) and \(84\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1201\)
B. \(1310\)
C. \(983\)
D. \(1092\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
156=2^{2}\times 3\times 13
\]

\[
84=2^{2}\times 3\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(156,84)=1092
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1092.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1092\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(156,84)=\frac{156\times 84}{\operatorname{HCF}(156,84)}=1092
\]

Fast result: \(1092\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q197. Between \(211\) and \(373\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 211 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 373.

\[
373=15\times 24+13
\]

\[
373-13=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{360-225}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{373}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{210}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-14=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q199. When \(13^{44}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(44-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{44}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(44-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
3^{44}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q204. The number \(53x50\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 13; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(65-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{65}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q227. The number \(832x0\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 13; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

Cycle length = 4.

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(94-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
3^{94}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(94-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
3^{94}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
10+3+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 3 dividing 30! is 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{3}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{9}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)

D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 215 is 216.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 318.

\[
318=12\times 26+6
\]

\[
318-6=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{312-216}{12}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{318}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{214}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(660\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
40=2^{3}\times 5
\]

\[
80=2^{4}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,80)=80
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 80.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(80\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,80)=\frac{40\times 80}{\operatorname{HCF}(40,80)}=80
\]

Fast result: \(80\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q252. Power \(7^{92}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(92-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
7^{92}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(92-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{92}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q262. Between \(204\) and \(309\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 204 is 210.

\[
15\times 14=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 309.

\[
309=15\times 20+9
\]

\[
309-9=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-210}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{309}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{203}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-13=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q275. After prime factorising \(18\), what is the product of all positive divisors?

B. \(6998\)
C. \(5249\)
D. \(5832\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
18=2\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(18)=(1+1)\times (2+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 18 d=18^{d(18)/2}=18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 5832.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5832\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Fast result: \(5832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(81-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{81}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(81-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
3^{81}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[

\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 40! = 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a cube, every exponent must be a multiple of 3.

\[
2^{1}\to 2^{3}
\]

\[
5^{3}\to 5^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
250\times 4=1000
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=1000
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1000\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=4
\]

Fast result: \(1000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q288. Between \(240\) and \(417\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(30\)
B. \(31\)
C. \(29\)
D. \(32\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 240 is 240.

\[
6\times 40=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 414 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 417.

\[
417=6\times 69+3
\]

\[
417-3=414
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{414-240}{6}+1=29+1=30
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 30.
Hence, there are 30 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(30\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{417}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{239}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
69-39=30
\]

Fast result: \(30\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q289. For two numbers \(75\) and \(255\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(1275\)
B. \(1403\)
C. \(1530\)
D. \(1147\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
75=3\times 5^{2}
\]

\[
255=3\times 5\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,255)=1275
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1275.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1275\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,255)=\frac{75\times 255}{\operatorname{HCF}(75,255)}=1275
\]

Fast result: \(1275\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q306. For exponent \(42\), what ending digit does \(12^{42}\) have?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
12\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(42-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
12^{42}\equiv 4\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(42-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
2^{42}\equiv 4\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q313. Between \(174\) and \(295\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 174 is 180.

\[
15\times 12=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 285 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 295.

\[
295=15\times 19+10
\]

\[
295-10=285
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{285-180}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{295}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{173}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-11=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q322. The number \(87x92\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=26
\]

\[
S+x=26+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
26+x=27
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
26+1=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q323. Two buses leave a stand every \(32\) and \(56\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(213\)
B. \(224\)
C. \(235\)
D. \(246\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
32=2^{5}
\]

\[
56=2^{3}\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(32,56)=224
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 224.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(224\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(32,56)=\frac{32\times 56}{\operatorname{HCF}(32,56)}=224
\]

Fast result: \(224\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{267}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{129}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-10=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q345. The number \(1x658\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

B. \(12597120\)
C. \(15116544\)
D. \(10077696\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[

\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 36 d=36^{d(36)/2}=36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q360. In \(40!\), find the exponent of \(5\) in its prime factorisation?
A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 5 dividing 40! is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[

\]

Q361. Between \(151\) and \(299\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 151 is 162.

\[
18\times 9=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 299.

\[
299=18\times 16+11
\]

\[
299-11=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{288-162}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{299}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{150}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
16-8=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q362. A large power \(11^{82}\) is divided by \(9\). What remainder should be written?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(82-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
11^{82}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(82-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

\[
2^{82}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a cube, every exponent must be a multiple of 3.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{3}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{3}
\]

\[
5^{1}\to 5^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=27000
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27000\).

Shortcut

\[
M=75
\]

Fast result: \(27000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

The 8th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(68-1)\bmod 10+1=8
\]

\[
2^{68}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q392. Between \(234\) and \(381\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 234 is 234.

\[
9\times 26=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 378 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 381.

\[
381=9\times 42+3
\]

\[
381-3=378
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{378-234}{9}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{381}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{233}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
42-25=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(32,80)=160
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 160.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(160\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(32,80)=\frac{32\times 80}{\operatorname{HCF}(32,80)}=160
\]

Fast result: \(160\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q416. Between \(205\) and \(380\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 205 is 216.

\[
18\times 12=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 378 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 380.

\[
380=18\times 21+2
\]

\[
380-2=378
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{378-216}{18}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{380}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{204}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-11=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q419. When \(2^{48}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(48-1)\bmod 12+1=12
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
2^{48}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 12th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(48-1)\bmod 12+1=12
\]

\[
2^{48}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q430. The number \(3x092\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 14; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q442. Between \(123\) and \(296\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 123 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 296.

\[
296=18\times 16+8
\]

\[
296-8=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{288-126}{18}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

\]

Q451. Between \(163\) and \(277\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 163 is 168.

\[
8\times 21=168
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 272 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 277.

\[
277=8\times 34+5
\]

\[
277-5=272
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{272-168}{8}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{277}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{162}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
34-20=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=520
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 520.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(520\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(520\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q463. The number \(1x870\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q483. The number \(392x6\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=20
\]

\[
S+x=20+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[
A=20
\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
20-x=11
\]

\[
x=9
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=9\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
20-9=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[
x=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(528\).

\]

Fast result: \(528\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+2=42
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+4=24
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-2|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-2|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_digit_interchange
---
Q497. What is the highest power of \(2\) that divides \(75!\)?
A. \(72\)
B. \(70\)
C. \(73\)
D. \(71\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 2 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{2}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{4}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{64}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
37+18+9+4+2+1=71
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 75! is 71.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(71\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{32}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{64}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(71\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q498. When \(11^{69}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(69-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
11^{69}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(69-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
1^{69}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_modular_arithmetic
---
Q499. By what least number should \(20\) be multiplied so that the product is divisible by \(120\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
g=\gcd(20,120)=20
\]

\[
x=\frac{120}{g}=6
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 6.
\[
N=6
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_minimum_multiplier
